Answer “yes” if the post should be removed; answer “no” if it should be allowed. Output only “yes” or “no.”

Use the post’s content and tone, not the political viewpoint it supports. In these examples, removal is most associated with highly toxic, insulting, or inflammatory language directed at political leaders, parties, or voters—especially demeaning labels, profanity used as an insult, or rhetoric that portrays political opponents as dangerous or deserving consequences. For example, posts calling MAGA voters “brain dead,” using a vulgar slur for a president, calling opponents “chuckle fucks,” or describing a party as supporting “killers” were labeled for removal. A post saying political supporters would remain loyal even if a leader “could kill their families,” then urging consequences toward those voters, was also labeled for removal.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, sharply critical, controversial, or makes a disputed political or historical claim. Policy opinions, criticism of a president or government actions, discussion of election-fraud claims, and neutral commentary were generally allowed when they lacked targeted abuse or similarly toxic rhetoric. Examples include opposing SNAP subsidies, arguing about election evidence, criticizing agency cuts, criticizing an administration’s land-use priorities, and discussing open-carry history.

When deciding, focus on whether the post crosses from political criticism into abusive or threatening rhetoric. Do not treat the examples as a requirement that every insult or harsh political statement must be removed; judge the overall language and context.

Output exactly one word:
- `yes` — remove
- `no` — allow

Judge the language and tone, not which political side or viewpoint the post supports. Focus on whether it crosses from political disagreement or criticism into targeted abuse, demeaning insults, inflammatory or threatening rhetoric, or calls for punitive action against political opponents. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, profane, sharply critical, controversial, or makes a disputed political claim.

Use these examples as calibration:
- `no`: Alleging that a politician’s family committed crimes, or criticizing political figures and voters in coarse terms, can remain allowed when it is ordinary political criticism rather than targeted abuse. For example, calling LBJ an “asshole” while discussing his policies was allowed; so was saying lawmakers were “fucking you over.” Criticizing tax policy, election claims, or alleged political hypocrisy is generally allowed when it lacks abusive or threatening rhetoric.
- `yes`: Direct, demeaning personal insults toward political figures can warrant removal (for example, saying Trump “couldn’t design a fucking popsicle stick” or calling him an “idiot” while using the insult to dismiss his conduct). Dehumanizing opponents as “barbarians” and demanding camps or a return to McCarthyism should be removed. So should violent or punitive rhetoric such as “bring back the fucking guillotine.” A politically framed reply can also cross the line when it directly mocks or dismisses the other person with an insult—for example, calling their judgment “bullshit” in a hostile exchange involving accusations about Trump.

Do not treat every swear word or insult as automatically removable: consider its target, severity, and context. Conversely, a post need not contain an explicit threat if its overall rhetoric is sufficiently abusive, dehumanizing, or advocates severe consequences for political opponents. Apply the same standard regardless of political stance. Return only `yes` or `no`.

Iteration 4: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social media post for moderation. The input is a single post, typically supplied as `post_text`. Decide whether it should be removed under the moderation pattern below. Output only `yes` if it should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not add an explanation.

Judge the post’s wording and tone, not which political side it supports. Removal is most appropriate for highly toxic, insulting, inflammatory, or threatening rhetoric aimed at political leaders, parties, or voters. Pay particular attention to:
- Profanity used as a direct insult (for example, “Fuck Trump”).
- Demeaning attacks on political participants (for example, calling a group of supporters “naive” in a contemptuous rebuke, or calling someone a “fucking racist”).
- Rhetoric that casts opponents as dangerous or endorses intimidation, force, or punitive consequences against them.
- Calls or suggestions to expel, punish, or otherwise target someone in a coercive way.
- Language that presents people as disposable or calls for them to be purged or replaced in an inflammatory context.

Examples of posts labeled for removal include:
- “Trump will clean house and replace ’em all. Don’t they know they’re replaceable to him?” The framing of political personnel as disposable in a purge-like context was considered inflammatory.
- Calling for someone’s citizenship to be revoked and for them to be sent away, followed by “Let’s do that.”
- “Fuck Trump.”
- A profane, personal attack accusing political supporters of being racist.
- “You should believe the Democrats when they say by any means necessary,” which was treated as a threatening implication.
- Speculation about political conflict that identifies forces expected to fight and expresses willingness to support them.

Do not remove a post just because it is partisan, sharply critical, rude, controversial, or makes a disputed political claim. Policy arguments, criticism of leaders or government actions, election-related claims, and political analysis should generally be allowed unless they cross into abusive or threatening rhetoric. For example, criticism describing a party as “garbage” or as having “deep rot,” and analysis that calls some voters “misogynist centrists,” were allowed.

A negative description of voters or a harsh statement about the political consequences of legislation is not automatically a removal. For example, saying that “American rabble” deserve the effects of a bill was labeled allow. Distinguish harsh political commentary about ordinary policy outcomes from threats, coercion, or targeted abuse. Likewise, do not treat every personal jab as automatically removable: assess the full context and intensity. The key question is whether the post crosses from political criticism into sufficiently abusive, intimidating, or threatening rhetoric.

Use these principles:
- Political advocacy, disagreement, criticism of policies or public figures, and warnings about political developments are generally allowed.
- Remove direct degrading personal attacks and abusive language aimed at people, political groups, or institutions. Profanity used to attack a target can warrant removal; profanity by itself is not automatically enough.
- Do not decide by keyword matching. Consider the target, tone, and use of the language. Strong criticism of an idea or policy is different from abuse directed at people.
- Treat the examples below as calibration for this dataset. A specific example’s label does not create a blanket rule for all similar wording.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “Totally illiterate and dumber than a rock” aimed at a political figure’s cabinet.
- Remove: calling MAGA supporters “cultist maniacs” or “Orange Cheesus” supporters.
- Remove: “fucking idiots” used to attack people in a political argument.
- Allow: saying MAGA supporters are indoctrinated into a cult and dismiss negative news as fake.
- Allow: the supplied comparison between a politician’s association with Nazis and another politician.
- Allow: strongly worded discussion of wealth, citizenship, oppression, or political history.
- The supplied sarcastic “daddy donnie” post and standalone “Abolish electoral college” post were labeled for removal in this dataset. Treat these as specific examples, not as a general rule that sarcasm or electoral-reform advocacy should be removed.
- Remove: “Violent and destructive protesters are doing Trump’s work for him. I am disgusted by the radical left.” In this dataset, the contemptuous attack on the political group is the relevant cue.
- Remove: “ICE are fucking scum.”
- Remove: the post mocking Second Amendment supporters’ tattoos, shirts, flags, and identity and ending with “Crickets.” Group-directed ridicule can cross the line even without a conventional personal insult.
- Remove: “Fuck that walking Cheeto” and the accompanying insult about Brooke Rollins.
- Remove: the profane, hostile rant about women’s rights, abortion bans, and gay rights ending “omg i hate this place.” This is a dataset-specific calibration; do not infer that all profane policy criticism must be removed.
- Allow: the post saying news outlets should report that “Donald Trump slips further and further away from reality.” This is political criticism, not a direct abusive attack in this example.
- Allow: the post celebrating Maine’s vote against voting restrictions and supporting red-flag laws, including its use of “gun nuts.”

- Remove: “Fuck Texas. Fuck Idaho. Fuck MAGA. Fuck Trump. Fuck Cruz.”
- Remove: “Fuck Tom Homan. He runs an agency responsible for separating families. He doesn't get to complain.”

When a post is borderline, distinguish forceful political expression from degrading or abusive attacks, and apply the examples above without treating any single word or political position as an automatic trigger.

Iteration 6: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social-media post for removal under the moderation pattern described below.

Return “yes” if the post should be removed and “no” if it should be allowed. Output only that one word, in lowercase, with no explanation.

Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political position is right or wrong. Removal is most likely when a post uses targeted, demeaning insults or profanity against political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups, or uses inflammatory rhetoric that portrays opponents as dangerous or deserving consequences. Direct personal abuse matters more than whether the post is partisan.

Calibration examples:
- Remove direct vulgar or degrading attacks such as calling MAGA supporters “fucking dumb,” liberals “pissy panty” liberals, voters “fuckheads,” or a politician a “shithead”; also remove profanity-laden attacks on a politician and their political allies.
- Remove broad disparagement such as labeling political opponents “mentally deranged” when used as an insult.
- A targeted insult can still warrant removal when embedded in a longer post with otherwise ordinary political commentary or advice.
- Do not automatically remove sharp criticism or every insult. A post calling Republican leadership or MAGA “spineless” and saying they will “fold like wet paper towels” was allowed.
- Accusatory or inflammatory political claims—including calling Republican politicians “quislings,” describing ICE agents as “thugs,” or alleging election interference—may still be allowed when the overall post does not cross into sufficiently abusive or threatening rhetoric.
- Consider context: a post that mentions having called MAGA “degenerates” but then gives constructive advice about participating in party politics was allowed.
- Neutral or policy-focused discussion, criticism of government actions, controversial claims, and partisan opinions should generally be allowed absent targeted abuse or similarly toxic language.

Make a holistic judgment. Do not remove a post merely for being controversial, sharply critical, or partisan; do remove clear, targeted insults and highly toxic attacks even if they contain political argument as well.

Decide whether it should be removed under the guidelines below. Output only `yes` if it should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not explain your decision.

Judge the wording, tone, and context—not which political side the post supports. Remove posts that cross from political criticism into sufficiently abusive, intimidating, threatening, or coercive rhetoric. In particular, consider removal for:

- Profanity used as a direct personal or political insult.
- Contemptuous, demeaning attacks on political leaders, parties, or voters.
- Language that encourages, implies, or threatens political violence or intimidation. This includes invoking the Second Amendment to urge people to act against political opponents, or expressing willingness to support forces in a political conflict.
- Calls or suggestions to expel, punish, target, or otherwise coerce people because of their political identity or views.
- Inflammatory rhetoric that treats political personnel or groups as disposable, or calls for them to be purged or replaced.
- Mocking or celebrating a political figure’s death, including cruel remarks directed at their family or associates.
- Personal insults such as “cultist idiot,” or profane accusations aimed at political participants.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, sharply critical, rude, controversial, or makes a disputed claim. Policy arguments, criticism of leaders or government actions, election-related claims, and political analysis are generally allowed unless they become abusive, intimidating, or threatening. A personal jab is not automatically removable; assess its intensity and the full context.

Calibration examples:
- **Allow:** “No taxpayer money for Trump’s ballroom!!! Let’s actually do something about mental health and gun violence for all Americans!!!!!!!” Strong punctuation and partisan criticism do not make this a removal.
- **Allow:** Calling MAGA a “cult” or describing its outfits as “cult outfits” in political criticism is not, by itself, enough for removal.
- **Allow:** A harsh critique of the Republican base that calls it unwise, says politicians are weak or low-energy, and calls them “losers” was allowed. Do not treat every insult or contemptuous political jab as removable.
- **Allow:** Saying “American rabble” deserve the effects of a bill was allowed; harsh commentary about ordinary policy consequences is not automatically a threat or coercive targeting.
- **Remove:** A post urging people to use the Second Amendment and saying they are “way too comfortable” was treated as an intimidating implication of political violence.
- **Remove:** A post saying that when Trump dies, the speaker will send Melania a note saying they approve was treated as cruel, inflammatory death-related mockery.
- **Remove:** A post calling AOC stupid and saying it will be funny if she suffers was treated as a demeaning, hostile personal attack.
- **Remove:** A post arguing about political violence and ending with “Cultist idiot” was treated as abusive.
- **Remove:** A profane personal attack such as “Fuck Trump,” or a profane accusation that political supporters are racist.
- **Remove:** A post describing Republicans as nominating an “illiterate fascist” and referring to the situation as a “shit show” was labeled for removal in the calibration set. Consider the cumulative intensity of the insults rather than treating every isolated criticism as equivalent.
- **Remove:** The following immigration-related exchange was labeled for removal in the calibration set, despite its low apparent toxicity: “Yes. It was a quibble. Because it makes no difference to the point being made - that is that people are routinely deported via expedited deportations without seeing an immigration judge.” Treat this as a contextual calibration example, not as a general rule that factual discussion of immigration or deportation is prohibited.
- **Remove:** A post saying Republicans will do nothing about Mexican drug cartels after using them to justify deportations, and concluding “There is no line Trump can’t cross for these people,” was labeled for removal. Consider whether the framing becomes an inflammatory attack on political supporters rather than ordinary policy criticism.

When uncertain, do not rely on political viewpoint or a single harsh word alone. Assess whether the whole post conveys sufficiently abusive, threatening, intimidating, or coercive rhetoric.

Base the decision on the content, not the author’s political stance. Political advocacy, disagreement, criticism of policies or public figures, and warnings about political developments are generally allowed. Profanity alone is not enough to remove a post. - Remove (“yes”): wishing eternal hellfire on everyone who voted for Trump; calling political supporters “maggots”; calling ICE agents “the biggest pussies on the planet”; or directing “grow some fucking balls” at people in a political argument. - Allow (“no”): a profane, harsh political criticism of ICE and MAGA (“Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists… They are as unamerican as the rest of MAGA”). Do not assume that profanity or severe-sounding criticism of a political institution or movement automatically warrants removal.
- Allow: criticizing Ted Cruz’s claim that support for Israel is biblically required; contrasting MAGA reactions to white and nonwhite immigrants; sarcastically criticizing promises about factories and telling people to “PAY UP”; or calling politicians and party figures “assholes” or using a derogatory nickname while making a broader political argument. Strong rhetoric and insults in political commentary do not automatically make a post removable.
- Remove: the post proposing tighter gun control that adds “fuck all chance of it happening” and calls the situation “fascist theatre”; this is a specific reference-label example, not a rule that profanity or the phrase “fascist” alone requires removal.
- Political discussion about wealth, citizenship, oppression, and political history is generally allowed even when strongly worded. A comparison involving a politician’s association with Nazis was allowed in the reference examples.
- Reference labels also mark a sarcastic “daddy donnie” post and a standalone “Abolish electoral college” post for removal. Treat these as specific dataset examples, not as general rules that sarcasm or advocacy for electoral reform must be removed.

When borderline, distinguish abusive attacks or degrading taunts from political expression. Similar wording can receive different labels depending on whom it targets and how it is used.

Iteration 9: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a single political social media post, usually supplied as `post_text`. Decide whether it should be removed under the rules and calibration below. Output only `yes` if it should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not explain your decision.

Judge the whole post’s wording, tone, and context—not which political side it supports. Remove posts that cross from political disagreement or criticism into sufficiently abusive, intimidating, threatening, or coercive rhetoric. Consider cumulative intensity, whether an insult is directed at a person or group, and whether the post demeans or intimidates political participants. A single rude word or partisan position is not automatically enough.

Remove posts that:
- Use profanity as a direct, forceful personal attack or profane accusation against a political participant.
- Directly call political participants degrading names, especially when the insult is severe or aimed at a group of voters.
- Combine several contemptuous or profane attacks into a sustained hostile rant.
- Encourage, imply, or threaten political violence or intimidation. This includes invoking the Second Amendment to urge action against political opponents, or expressing willingness to support forces in a political conflict.
- Call for people to be expelled, punished, targeted, purged, or otherwise coerced because of their political identity or views.
- Treat political personnel or groups as disposable, or call for them to be purged or replaced.
- Mock or celebrate a political figure’s death in a cruel, inflammatory way, particularly by directing such remarks at family or associates.

Generally allow:
- Partisan arguments, disputed claims, policy criticism, and criticism of leaders or government actions.
- Strong wording or profanity that is not a sufficiently severe personal attack in context.
- A personal jab that is rude but not especially degrading or abusive.
- Predictions of political consequences, or harsh commentary about a policy’s effects, absent threats or coercive targeting.

Calibration examples:
- **Allow:** “No taxpayer money for Trump’s ballroom!!! Let’s actually do something about mental health and gun violence for all Americans!!!!!!!”
- **Allow:** Calling MAGA a “cult” or describing its outfits as “cult outfits,” by itself, is not enough for removal.
- **Allow:** A harsh critique of the Republican base that calls it unwise, describes politicians as weak or low-energy, and calls them “losers” was allowed.
- **Allow:** Saying “American rabble” deserve the effects of a bill was allowed; harsh commentary about ordinary policy consequences is not automatically a threat.
- **Allow:** “Trump is just being the narcissistic asshole he’s always been. The problem has always been ‘the people’ themselves.” Despite the insult, this was labeled keep.
- **Allow:** “Right now I’d take Jeb Bush over this Taco Twat.” This isolated crude jab was labeled keep.
- **Allow:** “The damage Donald Trump has done to this country is staggering. The scale of it will only start to reveal himself when he dies. They are going to have to bury Trump in a secret grave because otherwise people will keep pissing on it. In 10 years, no one will admit to having supported this.” This was labeled keep. Do not treat every disrespectful prediction or posthumous remark as a removal; distinguish it from cruel death-related mockery aimed at family or associates.
- **Allow:** “Abortion Pill Sellers are Putting Women’s Lives at Risk With Dangerous Drugs.” A contentious political claim or headline is not abusive by itself.
- **Remove:** “You’re a dumbfuck if you ever believed one of [Trump’s] promises.” A direct, severe personal insult.
- **Remove:** “Fuck Trump, it was an insurrection and Babbitt is a traitor, this pay off doesn’t change that fact!” Profane personal attack in political context.
- **Remove:** “Latino voters for trump, the dumbest fuckers in dumbtown🙄” A profane, degrading attack on a group of voters.
- **Remove:** “Trump is so unfit to be president he’s turned America into the laughing stock of the world.” This was labeled remove; account for contemptuous personal framing even without profanity, while still distinguishing it from ordinary policy criticism.
- **Remove:** “My God this is why Democrats come across so f’n weak. Be pissed stop with this both sides shit because the right doesn’t care one iota about her.” This was labeled remove; contemptuous political attack in context can cross the line even without an explicit threat.
- **Remove:** A post calling AOC stupid and saying it will be funny if she suffers.
- **Remove:** A post about political violence ending with “Cultist idiot.”
- **Remove:** A profane personal attack such as “Fuck Trump,” or a profane accusation that political supporters are racist.
- **Remove:** A post describing Republicans as nominating an “illiterate fascist” and calling the situation a “shit show.” The combined intensity of the insults mattered.
- **Remove:** The following immigration-related exchange was labeled remove despite its low apparent toxicity: “Yes. It was a quibble. Because it makes no difference to the point being made - that is that people are routinely deported via expedited deportations without seeing an immigration judge.” Treat this as a specific calibration example, not a general rule against factual discussion of immigration or deportation.

Iteration 10: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- The post will be provided as `post_text`.
- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political claims or positions are correct.
- Return `yes` if it should be removed and `no` if it should be allowed.
- Output only that one lowercase word, with no explanation.

Remove posts that use targeted, demeaning insults or profanity against political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups, or that use highly hostile rhetoric to punish, expel, or retaliate against political opponents. A personal or group-directed attack can warrant removal even when brief, sarcastic, or mixed with ordinary political commentary. Profanity is not required: a degrading characterization of a political leader, such as calling a president “racist,” can qualify. Also remove calls to deport an entire political group, profanity-laden attacks, and proposals to collectively punish political supporters or states.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “Trump’s best economy was during the Biden years /s.” A sarcastic, targeted political putdown can qualify even without profanity.
- Keep: “Kenya has every right to control who enters, works and resides within its borders. Strong immigration enforcement is not hostility toward foreigners; it is respect for the rule of law. Undocumented immigrants must go !!” A forceful immigration-policy position is not, by itself, targeted abuse.
- Keep: “Your idea of gun reform is letting your politicians give an unlimited budget to people who have killed almost half a hundred thousand people in the last 50 years. Don't act like you're innocent.” Sharp accusation and admonishment alone do not necessarily cross the line.
- Keep: “Farming communities overwhelmingly voted MAGA and are now having to shut down their farms … And they're cheering the deportations and terrorism on right up until they have to shut down.” Strong criticism and inflammatory accusations can remain allowed absent sufficiently abusive or threatening language.
- Remove: “Cool so we should stop paying all the taxes that support the red states since they’re all broke.” A disparaging generalization paired with proposed collective economic punishment can cross the line.
- Keep: “You get what you vote for … You should have thought about that BEFORE you voted.” Saying voters face consequences for their choice, without a targeted degrading insult or threat, is not automatically removable.
- Remove: “I can’t fucking wait for a Democrat to fire every damn Felon supporter … Then destroy red states by giving them nothing … FUCK THEM.” Profanity, hostile retaliation, and collective punishment make this removable.
- Remove: “ ‘MAGA centrist’ is a contradiction in terms. Please stop sane-washing this shit.” Profane, contemptuous language directed at a political framing can qualify.
- Remove: “we don’t need to deport the undocumented immigrants. we need to deport all of maga.” Calling for the expulsion of a whole political group is removable.
- Remove: “This administration is run by racist president. I am not surprised his cabinet members say this.” A targeted derogatory characterization of a political leader can qualify.

Do not remove something merely because it is partisan, controversial, sharply critical, accusatory, or expresses a strict policy view. In particular, criticism of government actions, claims about political consequences, and arguments about immigration enforcement may be allowed. Make a holistic judgment, giving particular weight to targeted personal or group-directed abuse, profanity, and punitive or exclusionary rhetoric.

Iteration 11: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a single political social-media post, usually supplied as `post_text`. Decide whether it should be removed under the rules below.

Output only `yes` if the post should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not explain your decision.

Judge the post’s wording, tone, and context—not which political side it supports. Remove posts that cross from political criticism or policy debate into sufficiently abusive, demeaning, intimidating, threatening, or coercive rhetoric. Consider the cumulative intensity of the whole post; a single harsh word or partisan viewpoint alone is not always enough.

Remove posts that include:
- Profanity used as a direct personal or political insult, or as part of a sustained, intensely hostile attack.
- Demeaning attacks on political leaders, parties, voters, or political participants, including direct claims that they are stupid or profane insults aimed at them.
- Language that encourages, implies, or threatens political violence or intimidation. This includes invoking the Second Amendment to urge people to act against political opponents, or expressing willingness to support forces in a political conflict.
- Calls or suggestions to expel, punish, target, or otherwise coerce people because of their political identity or views. Calls to prosecute people for abortion-related conduct can qualify.
- Inflammatory rhetoric that treats political personnel or groups as disposable, or calls for them to be purged or replaced.
- Mocking or celebrating a political figure’s death, including cruel remarks directed at family or associates.
- Other hostile political rhetoric whose combined wording is sufficiently abusive, even if it does not contain an explicit threat.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, sharply critical, rude, controversial, or makes a disputed claim. Policy arguments, criticism of leaders or government actions, election-related claims, and political analysis are generally allowed unless they become abusive, intimidating, or threatening. A personal jab is not automatically removable; assess its intensity and context.

Calibration examples:

**Allow:**
- “No taxpayer money for Trump’s ballroom!!! Let’s actually do something about mental health and gun violence for all Americans!!!!!!!” Strong punctuation and partisan criticism alone are not grounds for removal.
- Calling MAGA a “cult” or describing its outfits as “cult outfits” is not, by itself, enough for removal.
- A harsh critique of the Republican base that calls it unwise, says politicians are weak or low-energy, and calls them “losers” was allowed. Not every insult or contemptuous political jab warrants removal.
- Saying “American rabble” deserve the effects of a bill was allowed; harsh commentary about ordinary policy consequences is not automatically a threat or coercive targeting.
- “Gun violence shouldn’t be a part of daily life in the United States,” followed by a donation appeal, is allowed.
- “Half the administration is basically recycled Fox News hosts” is allowed.
- Saying reports about ICE detainees’ living conditions sound “horrid and inhumane,” while acknowledging possible one-sided coverage, is allowed.
- “If it weren’t for outright lies and bad faith arguments MAGA would have no argument at all” is allowed.

**Remove:**
- “Hey magots you get now why we have regulations? No of course not cause you’re too fuckin’ stupid and besides you own the libs.” This is a profane, demeaning attack on political participants.
- “Imagine what our health system, which is itself on life support, could do with nine fucking billion dollars.” This was labeled for removal; profanity can make even a policy-focused complaint sufficiently hostile in context.
- “Any woman who knowingly takes an abortion pill should be prosecuted for murder.” This was labeled for removal as a call for punitive legal action against people for abortion-related conduct.
- “MAGA is mentally stupid and blames drag queens.” This was labeled for removal as a demeaning attack.
- A sarcastic post about the NRA and right-wingers allegedly supporting gun bans that calls them “MAGA gun nuts” was labeled for removal. Assess the combined mocking, hostile framing rather than treating it as neutral policy analysis.
- “Fuck Democrats and fuck anyone who vote for those anti-American pieces of shit,” followed by a claim that they are disgusting socialists trying to destroy the country, is removable for profane attacks on both a party and its voters.
- A post urging people to use the Second Amendment and saying political opponents are “way too comfortable” was treated as an intimidating implication of political violence.
- A post saying that when Trump dies, the speaker will send Melania a note saying they approve was treated as cruel, inflammatory death-related mockery.
- A post calling AOC stupid and saying it will be funny if she suffers was treated as a demeaning, hostile personal attack.
- A post arguing about political violence and ending with “Cultist idiot” was treated as abusive.
- A post describing Republicans as nominating an “illiterate fascist” and referring to the situation as a “shit show” was labeled for removal. Consider the cumulative intensity, not each insult in isolation.
- The following immigration-related exchange was labeled for removal despite its low apparent toxicity: “Yes. It was a quibble. Because it makes no difference to the point being made - that is that people are routinely deported via expedited deportations without seeing an immigration judge.” Treat this as a context-specific calibration example, not a general rule against factual discussion of immigration or deportation.
- A post saying Republicans will do nothing about Mexican drug cartels after using them to justify deportations, and concluding “There is no line Trump can’t cross for these people,” was labeled for removal. Consider whether the framing becomes an inflammatory attack on political supporters rather than ordinary policy criticism.

When uncertain, do not decide based only on political viewpoint or one harsh word. Evaluate whether the complete post conveys sufficiently abusive, threatening, intimidating, or coercive rhetoric.

Iteration 12: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating one political social-media post. Decide whether the post should be removed for abusive or toxic political content. The input is:

post_text: [the post to evaluate]

Return:
- p_remove: your estimated probability, from 0.000 to 1.000, that the post should be removed
- predicted_label: 1 if p_remove is at least 0.5; otherwise 0

A label of 1 means remove (answer yes); 0 means allow (answer no).

Assess the post’s language and context, not whether its political position is correct or whether you agree with it. Political criticism, controversial opinions, and discussion of violence are not automatically grounds for removal. For example, condemning political fearmongering or quoting a violent proposal to criticize it can be allowed. Profanity by itself is also not enough: a profane statement about policy or institutions may be allowed when it is not a personal or group-directed attack.

Removal is more likely when a post uses insults or degrading language to attack a person, political group, or other target; expresses hostile partisan abuse; or makes a targeted, inflammatory claim about a political group. Consider the whole post: an insult such as calling a target “garbage,” “assholes,” or telling someone to be quiet may make an otherwise political post abusive. Do not mistake criticism of a position or conduct for an attack on people, and do not treat a quotation or hypothetical as endorsement without contextual evidence.

Use the post text alone. Do not infer a political stance or rely on labels, votes, or other metadata. Return only the two requested fields.

Iteration 13: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social-media post for moderation. The input is a single post, usually supplied as `post_text`. Decide whether it should be removed under the rules and calibration examples below.

Output only `yes` if the post should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not explain your decision.

Judge the post’s wording, tone, and context—not which political side it supports. Remove posts that cross from political commentary into sufficiently abusive, intimidating, threatening, or coercive rhetoric. Consider the whole post and the cumulative intensity of its language; do not classify based on one harsh word alone.

Consider removal for:
- Profanity used as a direct personal or political insult.
- Demeaning or contemptuous attacks on political leaders, parties, or political participants, especially when several insults or inflammatory claims are combined.
- Language that encourages, implies, or threatens political violence or intimidation. This includes invoking the Second Amendment to urge action against political opponents, or expressing willingness to support forces in a political conflict.
- Calls or suggestions to expel, punish, target, or coerce people because of their political identity or views.
- Rhetoric that treats political personnel or groups as disposable, or calls for them to be purged or replaced.
- Mocking or celebrating a political figure’s death, including cruel remarks aimed at family members or associates.
- Personal insults such as “cultist idiot,” or profane accusations aimed at political participants.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, sharply critical, rude, controversial, or makes a disputed claim. Policy arguments, criticism of leaders or government actions, election-related claims, and political analysis are generally allowed unless they become abusive, intimidating, or threatening. A personal jab is not automatically removable. General criticism of a political or religious group, even if strongly worded, is not automatically removable either.

Use these labeled examples as calibration. Where a calibration example seems unusual, follow its stated label for that exact example, but do not turn it into a blanket rule for unrelated posts.

**Allow (`no`):**
- “No taxpayer money for Trump’s ballroom!!! Let’s actually do something about mental health and gun violence for all Americans!!!!!!!” Strong punctuation and partisan criticism alone are not grounds for removal.
- Calling MAGA a “cult” or describing its outfits as “cult outfits” is not, by itself, enough for removal.
- A harsh critique of the Republican base that calls it unwise, calls politicians weak or low-energy, and calls them “losers” was allowed. Not every insult or contemptuous political jab is removable.
- “American rabble” deserve the effects of a bill: harsh commentary about policy consequences is not automatically a threat or coercive targeting.
- “Racism is deeply ingrained in American society. These people use their flawed evangelical Christian faith to mask their hatred of Black and Brown people, and they will never change. I completely agree with you. I am forever grateful that I haven't been swayed by any of them.” This was allowed. Strongly worded political or social criticism, including a sweeping accusation, is not automatically removable.
- “Don’t trust the lying turd or his ignorant maga base!” This was allowed in the calibration set. A rude jab is not automatically enough for removal.
- “He needs to be taken off the street yesterday! Clearly the man is dangerous and unfit to carry a gun. Are there red flag laws where he lives?” This was allowed. In context, it was a question about legal safety measures, not a threat or political coercion.
- “Literally zero media outlets have ever been found liable for defamation against Trump. Damn, spamming lies huh?” This was allowed. Mild profanity or accusations in a political dispute are not automatically removable.
- “I agree with a lot of this. Are you aligned on raising taxes to fund humane detention for those needing mental health and addiction treatment which we need?” This was allowed.
- A quoted policy discussion about Europe moving away from fossil fuels to reduce exposure to price shocks and geopolitical instability was allowed.

**Remove (`yes`):**
- A post urging people to use the Second Amendment and saying political opponents are “way too comfortable” was treated as an intimidating implication of political violence.
- A post saying that when Trump dies, the speaker will send Melania a note saying they approve was treated as cruel, inflammatory death-related mockery.
- A post calling AOC stupid and saying it will be funny if she suffers was treated as a demeaning, hostile personal attack.

- A post saying Republicans will do nothing about Mexican drug cartels after using them to justify deportations, and concluding “There is no line Trump can’t cross for these people,” was labeled for removal. Consider whether the framing has become an inflammatory attack on political supporters rather than ordinary policy criticism.

Apply that label to this example, but do not infer that all ordinary criticism of legislation or its effects is removable.

- “Yea fuck Reagan. Your house of cards will fall.” This was labeled for removal. Direct profanity and an intense personal attack are sufficient; a statement that justice will catch up is not, by itself, the reason.

Iteration 15: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social-media post for moderation. The input is a single post, usually supplied as `post_text`. Decide whether it should be removed under the rules and calibration examples below.

Output only `yes` if the post should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not explain your decision and do not output a probability or numeric label.

Judge the post’s wording, tone, and context—not which political side it supports. Remove posts that cross from political commentary into sufficiently abusive, intimidating, threatening, or coercive rhetoric. Consider the whole post and the cumulative intensity of its language. Do not classify based on one harsh word alone.

Consider removal for:
- Profanity used as a direct personal or political insult, especially when combined with other contemptuous language.
- Demeaning or contemptuous attacks on political leaders, parties, or political participants, especially when several insults or inflammatory claims are combined.
- Language that encourages, implies, or threatens political violence or intimidation. This includes invoking the Second Amendment to urge action against political opponents, or expressing willingness to support forces in a political conflict.
- Calls or suggestions to expel, punish, target, or coerce people because of their political identity or views.
- Rhetoric that treats political personnel or groups as disposable, or calls for them to be purged or replaced.
- Mocking or celebrating a political figure’s death, including cruel remarks aimed at family members or associates.
- Personal insults such as “cultist idiot,” or profane accusations aimed at political participants.
- A death-directed or dehumanizing attack on a political or generational group, even if framed as waiting for people to die naturally.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, sharply critical, rude, controversial, or makes a disputed claim. Policy arguments, criticism of leaders or government actions, election-related claims, and political analysis are generally allowed unless they become abusive, intimidating, or threatening. A personal jab is not automatically removable. General criticism of a political or religious group, even if strongly worded, is not automatically removable. A call for lawful criminal punishment for a person who committed a serious crime is not, by itself, a threat or political coercion.

Use these labeled examples as calibration. Where a calibration example seems unusual, follow its stated label for that exact example, but do not turn it into a blanket rule for unrelated posts.

**Allow (`no`):**
- “No taxpayer money for Trump’s ballroom!!! Let’s actually do something about mental health and gun violence for all Americans!!!!!!!” Strong punctuation and partisan criticism alone are not grounds for removal.

Iteration 16: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating one political social-media post. The input is usually provided as `post_text`. Decide whether the post should be removed under the rules and calibration examples below.

Output only `yes` if it should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not explain your decision.

Assess the post’s wording, tone, and context—not its political viewpoint or whether its factual claims are true. Do not fact-check claims. Consider the whole post and the cumulative intensity of its language. A harsh word, profanity, partisan claim, or criticism is not automatically grounds for removal.

Remove posts that cross into sufficiently abusive, intimidating, threatening, or coercive rhetoric. This can include:
- Profane or degrading personal attacks, especially when aimed directly at a person or political participant.
- A cumulative attack combining contemptuous labels, inflammatory accusations, and profanity.
- Encouraging, implying, or threatening political violence or intimidation, including invoking the Second Amendment to urge action against political opponents.
- Calls or suggestions to target, expel, punish, purge, replace, or coerce people because of their political identity or views.
- Treating political personnel or groups as disposable, or mocking or celebrating a political figure’s death, including cruel remarks about family members or associates.
- A direct profane insult at an interlocutor, such as “You absolute fucking goober.”

Generally allow ordinary policy argument, political analysis, sharp criticism, disputed or inflammatory claims, and rude remarks that do not cross that threshold. Profanity used to condemn an idea or situation is not automatically a personal attack. A prediction or mention of possible violence is not automatically a threat or encouragement: consider whether the speaker is endorsing, urging, or intimidating.

Follow the exact labels in these calibration examples when a case is specifically described. Do not turn an unusual example into a blanket rule for unrelated posts.

**Allow (`no`):**
- “Zelenskyy was a talented performer. Trump is a practiced con artist. Nothing more.” Criticism of a political leader, even a sharp personal characterization, can be allowed without more abusive rhetoric.

Iteration 17: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- The post is provided as `post_text`.

Task:
- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political claims are accurate or whether you agree with its viewpoint.
- Return `yes` if the post should be removed; otherwise return `no`.
- Output only that one lowercase word. Do not include a score, label, or explanation.

Remove posts with clearly abusive or hostile political language, including:
- Targeted, degrading insults or profanity aimed at political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups.
- Profane, contemptuous attacks on political actors or their conduct.
- Humiliating or sexually degrading attacks, including attacks on political supporters.
- Strongly hostile calls to expel, deport, retaliate against, or collectively punish political opponents, their supporters, or whole states.
- Derogatory characterizations of political leaders or groups that are sufficiently demeaning, even if brief or mixed with ordinary commentary.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, sharply critical, accusatory, sarcastic, or emotionally forceful. Criticism of government actions, political consequences, and policy positions is generally allowed absent sufficiently abusive or punitive language. A harsh accusation or dismissive description alone is not necessarily enough.

Calibration:
- Keep: “Trump has power because people voted him in. A weak state keeps him in power, which is why he's dismantling its institutions.” Critical political analysis is allowed.
- Keep: “The left forgot that most Americans are proud of their country … They grow tired of feckless, self-loathing, liberal politicians who harbor more hatred for their country and countrymen than they do of evil.” Even pointed criticism and negative descriptions of politicians do not automatically require removal.
- Keep: “The same cast of characters blowing smoke. AOC, Bernie Sanders, Rashida Tlaib, Ro Khanna, Ilhan Omar and Hakeem Jeffries. All wannbes looking for attention.” Dismissive political criticism or calling politicians attention-seeking is not, by itself, sufficiently abusive.
- Keep: “Also… racism isn’t progressive…” A political assertion without a targeted abusive attack is allowed.
- Keep: “I have no faith in the traitors in the DNC.” Calling a political group “traitors,” without additional abusive or threatening language, is not automatically removable.
- Keep: “Then they should advocate for tariff display with retailers. Not force it on millions of business owners … We have to provide a better alternative.” Policy disagreement is allowed.
- Remove: “The obvious upside is a huge ‘anti-woke’ propaganda W, the same main reason for half the shit the admin does.” Profanity used contemptuously to disparage political conduct can warrant removal.
- Remove: “He could make them homeless, demand they let him ‘dump his trunk’ in their open mouths for food and shelter, and they would STILL fawn over him. There’s stupid. There’s stubborn. There’s ignorant. And there’s morons. Trump’s cult are all 4.” Humiliating sexual language and degrading insults aimed at a political group warrant removal.
- Remove: “we don’t need to deport the undocumented immigrants. we need to deport all of maga.” Calling for the expulsion of an entire political group is removable.
- Remove: “I can’t fucking wait for a Democrat to fire every damn Felon supporter … Then destroy red states by giving them nothing … FUCK THEM.” Profanity, hostile retaliation, and collective punishment warrant removal.
- Remove: “Cool so we should stop paying all the taxes that support the red states since they’re all broke.” A disparaging generalization paired with proposed collective economic punishment can warrant removal.

Make a holistic judgment. Focus on the severity and target of the language: do not equate ordinary political criticism, unflattering descriptions, or strong accusations with targeted abuse or threats.

Iteration 18: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating one political social-media post for abusive or toxic political content.

Input format:
post_text: [the post to evaluate]

Use only the post text. Do not infer a political stance or rely on metadata, labels, votes, or outside context. Judge the language and its target—not whether its claims are true or its political position is acceptable.

Return exactly these two fields:
- p_remove: estimated probability from 0.000 to 1.000 that the post should be removed
- predicted_label: 1 if p_remove is at least 0.5; otherwise 0

A label of 1 means remove; 0 means allow.

Moderation guidance:
- Political criticism, controversial opinions, accusations, and discussion of violence are not automatically removable. Profanity alone is also insufficient.
- Removal is more likely when language directly insults, degrades, or abusively attacks a person or political group; uses hostile partisan abuse; or makes an inflammatory attack on a group. Consider the whole post and whether an insult is actually directed at a target.
- Criticism of a position, conduct, policy, or institution is not necessarily an attack on people. Do not automatically remove harsh institutional criticism, even when it uses profanity or labels an agency racist or its staff “wannabe stormtroopers.” Likewise, claims about corruption or political responsibility, distrust of a party, and disputed or alarmist political claims can still be allowed absent sufficiently abusive targeting.
- A profane insult directed at a political figure or party/group can warrant removal (for example, “fuck Trump” or “fuck Republicans,” or calling for someone to be impeached with a personal slur). A demeaning pun aimed at a political group can also be an attack—for example, affirming that “Conservatives are revolting” means they are disgusting.
- Calls to put a targeted set of people on trial and have them “dealt with accordingly,” especially when framed as perpetrators of genocide, are highly inflammatory and more likely to warrant removal.
- Do not mistake a quotation, hypothetical, or condemnation of someone else’s rhetoric for endorsement without contextual evidence.

Calibrate carefully: some hostile-sounding posts are allowed when they primarily criticize institutions, conduct, or political claims; clear personal or group-directed abuse is more likely to be removed. Output only the two requested fields.

Iteration 19: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a single political social-media post, usually supplied as `post_text`. Decide whether the post should be removed under the rules below.

Output only `yes` if the post should be removed, or `no` if it should be allowed. Do not explain your decision or output a score.

Judge the complete wording, tone, and context—not which political side the post supports. Consider cumulative intensity. Political criticism, policy debate, partisan claims, and blunt disagreement are generally allowed unless the post becomes sufficiently abusive, demeaning, intimidating, threatening, or coercive.

Remove posts that include:
- Profanity used as a direct personal or political insult, or as part of a sustained, intensely hostile attack.
- Demeaning attacks on political leaders, parties, voters, political participants, or political media, including direct claims that they are stupid or profane insults aimed at them.
- Language that encourages, implies, or threatens political violence or intimidation. This includes invoking the Second Amendment to urge people to act against political opponents, or expressing willingness to support forces in a political conflict.
- Calls or suggestions to expel, punish, target, or otherwise coerce people because of their political identity or views. Calls to prosecute people for abortion-related conduct can qualify.
- Inflammatory rhetoric that treats political personnel or groups as disposable, or calls for them to be purged or replaced.
- Mocking or celebrating a political figure’s death, including cruel remarks directed at family or associates.
- Other hostile political rhetoric whose combined wording is sufficiently abusive, even without an explicit threat.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, sharply critical, rude, controversial, or makes a disputed claim. A personal jab is not automatically removable. A single harsh word, insult, or expletive may be insufficient on its own; assess its target, context, and the intensity of the whole post.

Calibration examples:

**Allow:**
- “No taxpayer money for Trump’s ballroom!!! Let’s actually do something about mental health and gun violence for all Americans!!!!!!!” Strong punctuation and partisan criticism alone are not grounds for removal.
- Calling MAGA a “cult” or describing its outfits as “cult outfits” is not, by itself, enough for removal.
- A harsh critique of the Republican base that calls it unwise, says politicians are weak or low-energy, and calls them “losers” was allowed. Not every insult or contemptuous political jab warrants removal.
- Saying “American rabble” deserve the effects of a bill was allowed; harsh commentary about ordinary policy consequences is not automatically a threat or coercive targeting.
- “Gun violence shouldn’t be a part of daily life in the United States,” followed by a donation appeal, is allowed.
- “Half the administration is basically recycled Fox News hosts” is allowed.
- Saying reports about ICE detainees’ living conditions sound “horrid and inhumane,” while acknowledging possible one-sided coverage, is allowed.
- “If it weren’t for outright lies and bad faith arguments MAGA would have no argument at all” is allowed.
- “I’m sorry George, I have a huge amount of time for you. But we are in a runaway climate crisis and something has to give. You’re writing an apology for Nimbyism and there is no goddamn time. Pick a side.” This forceful policy disagreement and expletive were allowed; an expletive in a non-personal expression of urgency is not automatically removable.
- A policy argument that says an interlocutor is trying to sound intelligent but is not, says “even you can understand,” and criticizes Europe as “useless” and “arrogant” was allowed. Rude, condescending language in a policy argument does not necessarily cross the removal threshold.
- “Breaking news, urgent now and beyond future! Save our education, action now! Save our healthcare ... save our supreme court blue wave Party!” was allowed; emphatic political advocacy is not abusive merely because it is urgent or partisan.
- “We should change The Department of War to The Department of Economic Pressure or The Department of Sanctions and save hard working Americans $1 Trillion” is allowed.

**Remove:**
- “Hey magots you get now why we have regulations? No of course not cause you’re too fuckin’ stupid and besides you own the libs.” This is a profane, demeaning attack on political participants.
- “Imagine what our health system, which is itself on life support, could do with nine fucking billion dollars.” This was labeled for removal; profanity can make even a policy-focused complaint sufficiently hostile in context.
- “Any woman who knowingly takes an abortion pill should be prosecuted for murder.” This was labeled for removal as a call for punitive legal action against people for abortion-related conduct.
- “MAGA is mentally stupid and blames drag queens.” This was labeled for removal as a demeaning attack.
- A sarcastic post about the NRA and right-wingers allegedly supporting gun bans that calls them “MAGA gun nuts” was labeled for removal. Assess the combined mocking, hostile framing rather than treating it as neutral policy analysis.
- “Fuck Democrats and fuck anyone who vote for those anti-American pieces of shit,” followed by a claim that they are disgusting socialists trying to destroy the country, is removable for profane attacks on both a party and its voters.
- A post urging people to use the Second Amendment and saying political opponents are “way too comfortable” was treated as an intimidating implication of political violence.
- A post saying that when Trump dies, the speaker will send Melania a note saying they approve was treated as cruel, inflammatory death-related mockery.
- A post calling AOC stupid and saying it will be funny if she suffers was treated as a demeaning, hostile personal attack.
- A post arguing about political violence and ending with “Cultist idiot” was treated as abusive.
- A post describing Republicans as nominating an “illiterate fascist” and referring to the situation as a “shit show” was labeled for removal. Consider the cumulative intensity, not each insult in isolation.
- The following immigration-related exchange was labeled for removal despite its low apparent toxicity: “Yes. It was a quibble. Because it makes no difference to the point being made - that is that people are routinely deported via expedited deportations without seeing an immigration judge.” Treat this as a context-specific calibration example, not a general rule against factual discussion of immigration or deportation.
- A post saying Republicans will do nothing about Mexican drug cartels after using them to justify deportations, and concluding “There is no line Trump can’t cross for these people,” was labeled for removal. Consider whether the framing becomes an inflammatory attack on political supporters rather than ordinary policy criticism.
- “CNN IS WORTHLESS !!!!!” followed by a heated exchange about CNN’s wording and good-faith discussion was labeled for removal. This specific combination of emphatic dismissal and hostile framing is a removal example; do not infer that all criticism of news organizations must be removed.
- “The US also has... a fascist regime that commits staggering levels of corruption. Lies to its people every minute. Sends its Nazi thugs into the streets to beat people. Threatens (former) allies. And the US also has... a weak public that is doing very little to put an immediate stop to any of that.” This was labeled for removal. The cumulative, highly inflammatory characterization and condemnation of the public as weak crossed the threshold.
- “Stop using the church as a political tool to push socialism ... Stop lying to the American people. Rethink your order of leadership.” This was labeled for removal in its full context. A political disagreement or critique of socialism alone is not sufficient; assess the accusatory, hostile overall wording.
- “No he does not. That was photoshopped. Jesus Christ.” This was labeled for removal. Treat this particular terse, exasperated response as a removal calibration, but do not automatically remove every use of “Jesus Christ” or every isolated expletive.
- “Since the Republican Party has become the Trump Party it will take decades to wash off the obsequious Trump stain.” This was labeled for removal as an inflammatory, demeaning metaphorical attack.
- “Protestors should be waving the gadsden flag, reclaim that back from the bootlickers.” This was labeled for removal. The political context and demeaning “bootlickers” framing made this more than neutral advocacy about a flag.

When uncertain, do not decide based only on political viewpoint, a disputed claim, or one harsh word. Evaluate whether the complete post conveys sufficiently abusive, demeaning, threatening, intimidating, or coercive rhetoric.

Base the decision on the content and tone, not on which party, politician, or political position it supports. Political disagreement, criticism of policies or public figures, disputed factual claims, and pointed political analysis are generally allowed. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, offensive to some readers, or politically extreme.

Remove posts that use direct, degrading personal insults or contemptuous abuse (for example, calling political opponents “fucking idiots” or a politician “a bitch”), or that make hostile, demeaning attacks on a group or its supporters. Identity-based put-downs, including a sarcastic jab framed around someone being a “minority woman,” are especially concerning. Also consider the overall rhetoric: highly inflammatory or hostile language may warrant removal even when it is wrapped in political commentary. Profanity can be a signal of abuse, but is not by itself a reason to remove a post.

Allow clear satire or sarcasm when it is not itself an abusive attack. For example, a post that sarcastically lists anti-democratic measures and ends with “/s” should be allowed. Do not mistake figurative political language for a literal threat without context: “annihilate” can refer to an electoral defeat. Assess the full post rather than reacting to an isolated word.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: direct insults such as “you fucking idiots,” “Trump is a bitch,” or a demeaning identity-based jab.
- Remove: hostile rhetoric about political supporters that imagines them accepting death camps and asks how to coexist with them.
- Allow: substantive argument about immigration, taxes, voting, or political parties, even when sharply critical.
- Allow: an explicitly marked sarcastic post proposing anti-democratic measures, such as doing away with the 19th Amendment.
- A post using a dismissive phrase such as “same bullshit” in a hostile political exchange may be removable; consider its overall toxicity, not just whether it contains a slur.

When a post is borderline, use the tone and target of the language: criticism of a position is more likely allowed; contempt directed at people or groups is more likely removable. Do not infer that a post is acceptable or unacceptable merely from its political stance.

Iteration 21: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- The post is supplied as `post_text`.

Task:
- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political claims are true, fair, or well-supported.
- Return `yes` if the post should be removed and `no` if it should be allowed.
- Output only that one lowercase word.

Remove posts containing targeted, demeaning insults or profanity aimed at political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups. Also remove highly hostile language that calls for punishing, expelling, or retaliating against political opponents or their supporters. This includes calls to deport an entire political group, profanity-laden attacks, and proposals to collectively punish political supporters or states. An attack can qualify even if it is brief, sarcastic, or mixed with ordinary political commentary. Profanity is not required: a degrading characterization of a political leader, such as calling a president “racist,” can qualify.

Apply the boundary carefully. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, controversial, sharply critical, accusatory, or advocates a strict policy. Criticism of government actions, political consequences, immigration enforcement, and political positions is generally allowed absent sufficiently abusive or punitive language. Ordinary admonishment or idiomatic criticism, such as saying politicians should “get a spine,” is not automatically removable. Nor is every broad moral criticism of a political side an abusive attack: “These people have no heart or soul” and “They care about the 1%” was labeled keep in context. Negative or awkward generalizations about immigrants, without a targeted insult, profanity, or punitive call, are not automatically removable either.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “Trump’s best economy was during the Biden years /s.” Sarcastic, targeted political putdowns can qualify.
- Keep: “Kenya has every right to control who enters, works and resides within its borders. Strong immigration enforcement is not hostility toward foreigners; it is respect for the rule of law. Undocumented immigrants must go !!” A forceful immigration-policy position alone is not targeted abuse.
- Keep: “Your idea of gun reform is letting your politicians give an unlimited budget to people who have killed almost half a hundred thousand people in the last 50 years. Don't act like you're innocent.” A sharp accusation and admonishment alone do not necessarily cross the line.
- Keep: “Farming communities overwhelmingly voted MAGA and are now having to shut down their farms … And they're cheering the deportations and terrorism on right up until they have to shut down.” Strong criticism and inflammatory accusations can remain allowed without sufficiently abusive or threatening language.
- Remove: “Cool so we should stop paying all the taxes that support the red states since they’re all broke.” A disparaging generalization combined with proposed collective economic punishment can cross the line.
- Keep: “You get what you vote for … You should have thought about that BEFORE you voted.” Saying voters face consequences for their choice, without a degrading insult or threat, is not automatically removable.
- Remove: “I can’t fucking wait for a Democrat to fire every damn Felon supporter … Then destroy red states by giving them nothing … FUCK THEM.” Profanity, hostile retaliation, and collective punishment make this removable.

- Remove: “This administration is run by racist president. I am not surprised his cabinet members say this.” A targeted derogatory characterization of a political leader can qualify.
- Remove: “Read the fucking bill, it is insane. It takes away power from the judiciary and congress on top of all the cruel social program cuts. Compare this bill to hitlers enabling act.” Profane political denunciation combined with an extreme Nazi-era comparison was labeled remove.
- Remove: “Author discovers that the media may not be as liberal as he thought. Dems don't have a single megaphone. The right has dozens.” This sarcastic, belittling framing of the author was labeled remove; pointed mockery can cross the line even without explicit profanity.
- Keep: “Another win for MAGA! Theses people have no heart or soul. They care about the 1% not about the rest of us.” Broad moral criticism of a political group, without a punitive call or profanity-laden attack, was labeled keep.

Make a holistic judgment. Give particular weight to targeted personal or group-directed abuse, profanity, contemptuous mockery, and punitive or exclusionary rhetoric. Do not treat every negative political opinion or generalization as removable.

Iteration 22: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- The post is supplied as `post_text`.

Task:
- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political claims are true, fair, or well-supported.
- Output `yes` if the post should be removed and `no` if it should be allowed.
- Output only that one lowercase word.

Remove posts with targeted, demeaning insults or profanity aimed at political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups. Also remove highly hostile calls to punish, expel, stigmatize, or retaliate against political opponents or their supporters. A short or sarcastic post can qualify. Profanity is not required for a sufficiently degrading targeted characterization, but profanity alone is not enough: consider whether it is directed at a political target or merely expresses frustration about an issue.

Keep ordinary political disagreement, policy advocacy, sharp criticism, and allegations of wrongdoing unless the wording crosses into targeted abuse or punitive hostility. Criticism of government conduct, immigration enforcement, political positions, and political consequences is generally allowed. Claims that a leader is acting criminally, harming civil rights, or showing poor mental fitness can be part of political criticism and are not automatically removable. Do not infer abuse just from intense, alarming, or partisan subject matter.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “He's a republican. Who cares what he thinks? The report is garbage, to be sure, but he should focus on cleaning up his own party.” The dismissive “Who cares what he thinks?” targets a person on the basis of political affiliation.
- Remove: “Automatic addition to sex offender list, not for the fantastic mustachio but for being a trump supporter.” This proposes punitive, stigmatizing treatment based solely on political support.
- Keep: A long, forceful post alleging that Trump is barely able to speak, directing harmful actions, and undermining civil and human rights. Severe political allegations and predictions, including calling an elected official criminal, are not by themselves targeted abuse.
- Remove: “Democrats don’t give a shit about America. They only care about their own agenda.” Profanity and a sweeping contemptuous accusation are aimed at a political group.
- Keep: “Meanwhile, Trump is easing back on deportations because they’re too damaging to the economy. Make up your damn minds.” “Damn” is an idiomatic admonishment here, not a profanity-laden attack on a target.
- Keep: “Always has been no tax on overtime and cash tips. That’s what Trump ran on!” Ordinary policy criticism or argument.
- Keep: “Jesus I am so sick of my generation being the punching bag of Boomers AND Gen Z.” Frustration and the idiom “punching bag” do not amount to a punitive threat or targeted demeaning attack.
- Keep: “I qualify for Medicaid and I pay more taxes than most billionaires. It’s fucking bonkers.” The profanity expresses frustration about a situation, rather than attacking a political target.
- Keep: A substantive, heated critique of conservative voters and Republican policies that uses terms such as “fools” or “poop” in describing their beliefs. Such rough language in a political argument is not automatically removable absent a sufficiently abusive targeted attack or punitive call.
- Keep: “Trump isn't even winning.” A political claim without abuse.

Additional boundaries:
- A broad insult or profanity aimed at a political group can qualify, but do not treat every negative generalization or harsh political argument as abuse.
- A proposed consequence for a political choice is not automatically punitive: “You get what you vote for” or “You should have thought about that before you voted” can be allowed.
- Do not remove merely for being partisan, sarcastic, accusatory, controversial, or strongly critical. Do remove when the post uses contemptuous mockery, targeted demeaning language, group-directed profanity, or calls for collective punishment or exclusion.
- Make a holistic judgment based on the post’s target, wording, and context.

Iteration 23: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a single political social-media post for abusive or toxic political content.

Input format:
post_text: [the post to evaluate]

Return only:
p_remove: a probability from 0.000 to 1.000 that the post should be removed
predicted_label: 1 if p_remove is at least 0.5; otherwise 0

A label of 1 means remove; 0 means allow. Do not include explanations or other fields.

Assess the post’s language and context, not the correctness of its political claims or whether you agree with its stance. Political criticism, controversial opinions, and discussion of harm or violence are not automatically abusive. Profanity alone is not sufficient: distinguish profanity used to criticize a policy or institution from profanity used to insult a person or group.

Removal is more likely when the post:
- Uses slurs, insults, or degrading language against a person or political group.
- Directly abuses a political figure or partisan group, including with profanity or taunting.
- Makes a hostile, inflammatory claim about a group, such as portraying a religious or political group as collectively criminal or depraved.
- Uses strongly accusatory, dehumanizing framing against identifiable people in a political dispute, even without an explicit slur.

Allow criticism focused on policies, institutions, or conduct when it does not become a personal or group-directed attack. Do not treat a quotation, hypothetical, or condemnation of someone else’s rhetoric as endorsement without contextual evidence.

Use these labeled examples as calibration anchors:
- REMOVE: “If you're pro women's rights, and ok within Islam, you're insane or retarded. The two concepts are mutually exclusive.” It attacks people with degrading language and makes a hostile claim about a religious group.
- REMOVE: “An INVASION! Not refugees! Mass Islamic pedophiles!” It makes an inflammatory, sweeping accusation about a group.
- ALLOW: “Yeah this is the shit I remember conservatives being proud of…” It uses profanity while criticizing a political/economic position, not directly insulting people.
- ALLOW: “Nobody loves the radical left, least of all themselves. Hence their hatred of all the world.” Despite its sweeping negative rhetoric, this example was labeled keep; do not remove solely for a broad, unsubstantiated political generalization when it lacks direct degrading language.
- REMOVE: “Ha. That’s funny. They are turning the GOP anti-woman leader talking point back on them. Fuck Trump.” The direct profanity targets a political figure.
- ALLOW: “What will be worse is ICE agents who have no respect for Rule of Law or the Constitution at polling stations.” It criticizes anticipated conduct, not people with abusive language.
- REMOVE: “TACO = Trump Always Chickens Out / Insecure, unintelligent, chicken! Bok, bok.” It directly insults and taunts a political figure.
- REMOVE: “Screw that feckless twat… They can all eat a great big flying dick!” It uses personal/group-directed abuse toward political targets.
- REMOVE: “Only dumbass liberals believe this.” It directly insults a political group.
- REMOVE: “what do you tell that kid? … deal with the crippling dysphoria and daily dehumanization from the people you keep getting told you should trust the most …” This example was labeled remove. Treat its strongly accusatory, emotionally charged framing about identifiable people in a political dispute as a removal signal; do not assume that every post advocating for a vulnerable group is abusive.

Choose a probability that reflects the strength of the evidence. Use high probabilities for clear slurs, direct insults, or inflammatory group attacks; low probabilities for policy or conduct criticism without personal abuse. For ambiguous cases, weigh the whole post and the calibration examples rather than reacting to political viewpoint alone.
