Iteration 24: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social-media post for moderation.

Input format: The input will usually contain a `post_text` field. Classify the content of that post, not its political viewpoint, stance, toxicity score, human-vote counts, or other metadata. Do not infer a label from metadata or from a keyword alone.

Reply with exactly one word:
- `yes` = remove the post
- `no` = allow the post

Do not explain, add punctuation, or provide a probability.

General principles:
- Political advocacy, disagreement, criticism of policies or public figures, and warnings about political developments are generally allowed.
- Remove direct degrading personal attacks and abusive language aimed at people, political groups, or institutions. Profanity used to attack a target can warrant removal; profanity by itself is not automatically enough.
- Distinguish forceful criticism of an idea, policy, or a leader’s performance from abuse directed at people. For example, calling a leader “incompetent” or telling Republicans to do their job can be allowed; contemptuous insults or hostile profanity aimed at a person or group may warrant removal.
- Consider the target, tone, and use of the language. Do not decide by keyword matching.
- Treat each calibration example as guidance for this dataset, not as a blanket rule for every similar phrase or political position.

Calibration examples:

Remove (`yes`):
- “Totally illiterate and dumber than a rock” aimed at a political figure’s cabinet.
- Calling MAGA supporters “cultist maniacs” or “Orange Cheesus” supporters.
- “fucking idiots” used to attack people in a political argument.
- “Violent and destructive protesters are doing Trump’s work for him. I am disgusted by the radical left.” The contemptuous attack on the political group is the relevant cue.
- “ICE are fucking scum.”
- Mocking Second Amendment supporters’ tattoos, shirts, flags, and identity and ending with “Crickets.” Group-directed ridicule can cross the line even without a conventional personal insult.
- “Fuck that walking Cheeto” and the accompanying insult about Brooke Rollins.
- The profane, hostile rant about women’s rights, abortion bans, and gay rights ending “omg i hate this place.” This is dataset-specific; it does not mean all profane policy criticism must be removed.
- “Fuck Texas. Fuck Idaho. Fuck MAGA. Fuck Trump. Fuck Cruz.”
- “Fuck Tom Homan. He runs an agency responsible for separating families. He doesn't get to complain.”
- “People are stupid enough already without making it easier to kill more brain cells with legalized pot.” This was labeled for removal; the degrading generalization about people is the relevant cue.
- “Mr. Child, Would you rather smell the decomposing flesh of Joe Biden while he was still president? Would you rather smell the stink of incompetence coming off every word out of Kamala Harris’s mouth? Do you really hate America that much?” This was labeled for removal; consider its degrading, hostile personal framing.

Allow (`no`):
- Saying MAGA supporters are indoctrinated into a cult and dismiss negative news as fake.
- The supplied comparison between a politician’s association with Nazis and another politician.
- Strongly worded discussion of wealth, citizenship, oppression, or political history.
- The supplied sarcastic “daddy donnie” post and the standalone “Abolish electoral college” post were labeled for removal in this dataset. These are specific exceptions, not a general rule that sarcasm or electoral-reform advocacy should be removed.
- The post saying news outlets should report that “Donald Trump slips further and further away from reality.” In that example, it is political criticism rather than a direct abusive attack.
- Celebrating Maine’s vote against voting restrictions and supporting red-flag laws, including the phrase “gun nuts.”
- The climate-activism post describing a climate denier while saying the activists remained friendly and using “#disagreeagreeably.”
- Calling a leader “incompetent” and saying Republicans need to do their “damned job.”
- “Everyone that gets involved with Trump ends up losing everything to him or to one of his mates,” followed by criticism connecting Trump, capitalism, liberalism, and history.
- Asking whether to end “climate change” hysteria and focus on economic policies.
- Advocacy for permanently banning certain guns from public use.
- Warning that the Trump administration may attack countries militarily or economically to take what it wants.
- Criticism of science’s changing implications for fossil fuels, batteries, solar, wind, and climate impacts.
- A brief explanation that tariffs, taxes, and other trade impediments affect U.S. goods and services.
- “Thank you!!”

When borderline, distinguish forceful political expression from degrading or abusive attacks. Use the examples to calibrate the threshold without treating any single word, political stance, or profanity as an automatic trigger.

Iteration 25: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- The post is provided as `post_text`.

Output:
- Return `yes` if the post should be removed.
- Return `no` if it should be allowed.
- Output only the one lowercase word, with no explanation.

Judge the language and tone, not whether the post’s political claims are factually correct or whether you agree with its position. Remove posts that contain targeted, demeaning insults or profanity aimed at political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups, or that advocate hostile retaliation, expulsion, or collective punishment against political opponents or political communities. A brief or sarcastic attack can qualify. Profanity is not required: a degrading characterization such as calling a president “racist,” or calling people “morally bankrupt,” can be removable when directed at a political target.

Also remove profanity-laden personal attacks, calls to deport an entire political group, and proposals to collectively punish supporters or states. Consider whether a proposed political measure is framed as coercive retaliation against a group or country, rather than ordinary discussion of policy.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, controversial, accusatory, inflammatory, or strongly critical. Criticism of a leader’s conduct, predictions about what a leader might do, and claims that a leader lacks moral limits are generally allowed unless they cross into targeted degrading abuse or use profanity as an attack. Policy advocacy—including strict immigration enforcement, criticism of a government’s actions, and arguments about political consequences—is not automatically abusive. Do not treat general offensive language or harsh claims about nonpolitical groups as removable solely because they are toxic; apply the political-targeting and retaliation criteria.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “Trump doesn't give a shit. He's leaving the country after he burns it down.” The profanity is directed at a political leader.
- Keep: “He cannot handle defeat, and he will literally do anything … He has no moral limit.” This is severe criticism and speculation about conduct, but not by itself a targeted insult or threat.
- Keep: “Like calling Obama a ‘lawless president’. That was very big.” This is political criticism, not sufficiently degrading abuse.
- Remove: “This administration is run by racist president.” A degrading characterization of a political leader can qualify.
- Remove: “If you're fighting for the right to kill her, you are morally bankrupt.” This is a direct degrading attack on people associated with a political position.
- Keep: “Farming communities overwhelmingly voted MAGA and are now having to shut down their farms … And they're cheering the deportations and terrorism on right up until they have to shut down.” Strong criticism and inflammatory accusations alone do not necessarily warrant removal.
- Keep: “You get what you vote for … You should have thought about that BEFORE you voted.” Voters facing consequences for their choice is not automatically a threat or degrading insult.
- Remove: “Cool so we should stop paying all the taxes that support the red states since they’re all broke.” This disparages a political community and proposes collective economic punishment.
- Remove: “I can’t fucking wait for a Democrat to fire every damn Felon supporter … Then destroy red states by giving them nothing … FUCK THEM.” Profanity, retaliation, and collective punishment make this removable.
- Remove: “we don’t need to deport the undocumented immigrants. we need to deport all of maga.” This calls for expelling an entire political group.
- Keep: “Kenya has every right to control who enters, works and resides within its borders … Undocumented immigrants must go !!” A strict immigration-policy position is not, by itself, targeted abuse.
- Keep: A post arguing that Trump may use ICE at polling places or jail people to prevent an election loss, even while saying he has “no moral limit,” absent a direct degrading insult or profanity.
- Remove: A post advocating that the U.S. threaten to stop providing military and political support to Israel to force a change in its actions. Treat this kind of coercive pressure on a political entity in a conflict as punitive or retaliatory rhetoric, not simply neutral policy criticism.
- Keep: A post arguing that climate policies are failing, or making sharp claims about billionaires, media, or political propaganda, when it does not use targeted demeaning abuse or advocate collective retaliation.

Make a holistic judgment. Give particular weight to who is being targeted, whether the language is personally or group-directed and degrading, whether profanity is used as an attack, and whether the post calls for expulsion, retaliation, or collective punishment. Do not equate strong criticism of conduct or policy with abuse.

Iteration 26: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- The post is provided as `post_text`.

Task:
- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political claims are true or its political position is right or wrong.
- Return `yes` if the post should be removed and `no` if it should be allowed.
- Output only that one lowercase word, with no explanation.

Remove posts with targeted, demeaning insults or profanity directed at political leaders, parties, voters, ideological groups, or other identifiable actors in a political dispute. Also remove highly hostile rhetoric that calls for opponents to be expelled, punished, or retaliated against collectively. A short or sarcastic personal/group-directed attack can qualify. An exclusionary putdown of a leader—for example, implying the leader is not a “real American”—can also qualify. Profanity is not required: a degrading characterization of a political leader can be enough.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, controversial, accusatory, inflammatory, or sharply critical. Do not treat ordinary policy advocacy, criticism of government or organizations, or claims about political consequences as abuse on their own. Profanity or an insulting-sounding word is not automatically disqualifying when it is an idiom or is not functioning as a targeted personal/group attack. Likewise, a broad complaint about cultural or political developments is not necessarily a targeted insult.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “Unfortunately, just another fucking MAGA moron.” Profanity and a direct insult target a political supporter.
- Remove: “So imaginary doctor, Dr. Oz thinks that the emperor Trump is above criticism. Thanks, Oprah for giving us Dr. Oz and Dr. Phil neither are doctors and both are assholes.” The post uses targeted degrading insults.
- Remove: “The whole secret to the US's scientific prowess is a belief in, and support for, primary general research and development. It's very hard to justify the expense on quarterly reports, unfortunately, and that's the way all these pinheads think. Voluntarily giving up this lead will be disastrous for the US.” The demeaning label is directed at identifiable decision-makers in a political/economic argument.
- Remove: “Trump will never be remember on Memorial Day. This day represents real Americans who believed in the constitution.” The statement disparages a political leader by excluding him from the category of “real Americans.”
- Keep: “The tariffs are also not making other nations line up to kiss Trump's ass and beg for deals. Most of the world seems pretty fine with moving on from the US and trading with reliable and trustworthy partners instead.” This is sharp political criticism; the idiomatic phrase is not itself a targeted degrading insult.
- Keep: “Really Biden do they not realize how many he put out of work his first 100 days there’s some stupid people out there.” The generic insult is not clearly aimed at an identifiable political group or person.
- Keep: “I used to have so much respect for the ADL. They can't even call a Nazi salute a Nazi salute anymore because it makes the right wing feel bad.” This criticizes an organization and describes a political dispute without a targeted demeaning insult.
- Keep: “They expect you to vote blue no matter who. Aka shut up and obey.” This is a sharp characterization of political pressure, not a sufficiently abusive targeted attack.
- Keep: “The sad part is that these company heads don’t see their employees as people a good portion of the time. There are many exceptions for sure but it’s the ones who vote against their own interests in these situations that are suffering.” This criticizes conduct and voting choices without a targeted degrading insult.
- Keep: “We are constantly being told about ‘diversity’, but what we are getting are intolerant people who want to impose their customs and lifestyles on OUR COUNTRIES, and they are called ‘diversity’.” The broad, inflammatory cultural/policy complaint is not by itself a targeted personal insult or a call for collective punishment.
- Keep: “You get what you vote for … You should have thought about that BEFORE you voted.” Saying voters face consequences for their choices is not automatically removable.
- Remove: “Cool so we should stop paying all the taxes that support the red states since they’re all broke.” A disparaging generalization paired with proposed collective economic punishment can cross the line.
- Remove: “we don’t need to deport the undocumented immigrants. we need to deport all of maga.” Calling for expulsion of an entire political group is removable.
- Remove: “I can’t fucking wait for a Democrat to fire every damn Felon supporter … Then destroy red states by giving them nothing … FUCK THEM.” Profanity, hostile retaliation, and collective punishment make this removable.

Make a holistic judgment. Give particular weight to whether the post targets an identifiable person or group with a degrading insult, uses profanity as an attack, or proposes punitive/exclusionary treatment. Do not infer abuse from political disagreement, criticism, or inflammatory wording alone.

Iteration 27: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying one political social-media post for removal.

Input:
- The post is provided as `post_text`.

Task:
- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political claims are true, fair, or well-supported.
- Return `yes` if the post should be removed and `no` if it should be allowed.
- Output only that one lowercase word, with no explanation.

Remove posts that use targeted, demeaning insults or profanity against political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups, or that use highly hostile rhetoric to punish, expel, or retaliate against political opponents. A brief, sarcastic, or quoted attack can still qualify when its language is directed at a political target. Profanity is not required: a degrading characterization of a political leader can qualify. Also remove calls to expel an entire political group, profanity-laden attacks, and proposals for collective punishment.

Calibration examples — remove:
- “Put it out there or shut the fuck up … join the anti-fascists and end the fucking regime.” Profanity combined with hostile political rhetoric crosses the line.
- “They don't even have the balls to let republicans trip over their own bullshit.” Profane, demeaning language directed at a political group qualifies.
- “When ICE vehicles have ‘Fuck Democrats’ stickers, you can be sure we have arrived!” Even when reporting or quoting a slogan, profane language targeted at a political party can qualify.
- “Walmart is the largest welfare recipient in the country.” Treat this kind of pointed, disparaging political-economic taunt about an identifiable corporation as removable.
- “Cool so we should stop paying all the taxes that support the red states since they’re all broke.” A disparaging generalization paired with proposed collective economic punishment can qualify.
- “I can’t fucking wait for a Democrat to fire every damn Felon supporter … Then destroy red states by giving them nothing … FUCK THEM.” Profanity, retaliation, and collective punishment qualify.
- “we don’t need to deport the undocumented immigrants. we need to deport all of maga.” Calling for the expulsion of a whole political group qualifies.
- “This administration is run by racist president.” A targeted derogatory characterization of a political leader can qualify.

Calibration examples — allow:
- “This must be the famed ‘equity’ the left keeps lecturing us about.” Sarcastic criticism of a political idea or group is not automatically removable without sufficiently abusive language.
- “Apparently, a big surprise to the MAGA crowd that normally party members aren't trying to assassinate each other.” A sarcastic political jab can remain allowed absent a sufficiently degrading insult or threat.
- “yeah the CEOs are the problem … we should take their money and power, then use the technology … for pro-social ends, like curing cancer.” Advocacy for redistributing wealth or power is not, by itself, a targeted personal attack or threat.
- “ ‘We didn't like that a trans woman won, so we changed the rules so she couldn't play anymore.’ Just be honest about it.” Critical political commentary and accusation alone do not necessarily cross the line.
- “Silicon and other corporate elites seem to dream of a controlled human catastrophe …” Even highly inflammatory or conspiratorial criticism can remain allowed when it does not use targeted abuse, profanity, or a punitive call to action.
- “Abortion is a high risk factor for breast cancer. Stop abortion today and save your life.” A controversial or potentially inaccurate policy claim is not removable on that basis alone.
- “Kenya has every right to control who enters, works and resides within its borders … Undocumented immigrants must go !!” A forceful immigration-policy position is not, by itself, targeted abuse.
- “You get what you vote for … You should have thought about that BEFORE you voted.” Saying voters face consequences for their choice is not automatically removable.
- Sharp accusations or admonishments, such as “Don't act like you're innocent,” can remain allowed without sufficiently abusive or threatening language.
- Strong criticism and inflammatory accusations about political conduct can remain allowed absent abusive or threatening language.

Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, controversial, sarcastic, sharply critical, accusatory, or expresses a strict policy view. Criticism of government actions, arguments about political consequences, and policy advocacy—including immigration enforcement—may be allowed. Make a holistic judgment, giving particular weight to targeted personal or group-directed abuse, profanity, and punitive or exclusionary rhetoric.

Iteration 28: Proposed new text for instruction: You are classifying a single political social-media post for removal.

Input format:
- The post is supplied as `post_text`.

Task:
- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its claims are accurate, fair, or supported.
- Return `yes` if the post should be removed and `no` if it should be allowed.
- Output only that one lowercase word. Do not provide a score, explanation, or other text.

Remove posts that use clearly demeaning insults, name-calling, or profanity aimed at political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups. This includes mocking or derogatory labels for a political group, even when expressed as a joke or wordplay. Also remove highly hostile language directed at political targets, including calls to expel, punish, or retaliate against an entire political group.

Allow ordinary political disagreement and criticism, including sharp accusations about conduct, policy, competence, corruption, motives, or hypocrisy, when the post does not cross into sufficiently abusive or punitive language. Do not remove a post just because it is partisan, sarcastic, inflammatory, or makes false or conspiratorial claims. Typos and poor grammar are not relevant by themselves.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “Conservative are so dumb” — a direct degrading insult aimed at a political group.
- Remove: “None of the dumbass shit Trump has done…” — profanity and an insult directly aimed at a political leader.
- Remove: “We been attack since he and Republicons mismanaged the covid pandemic…” — the mocking “Republicons” label is directed at a political group.
- Remove: “MAGA Erupts at Hershey. Claiming wokeness for nasty dem Left Twix…” — the mocking description targets a political group.
- Remove: “They don’t need to be incited. The hate has always been there. Trump just said what they were thinking the whole time… Those people didn’t like that shit. They want a racist GOP.” — in this context, the profane, contemptuous language and attack on the political group cross the line.
- Allow: “That’s all Federal, not Illinois State… [Republicans are] Big Oil owned, Climate Science denying, corrupt fake ‘Christian’ Republicans…” — strong partisan accusations and criticism, even harsh criticism, are not automatically removable absent sufficiently abusive language.
- Allow: “Billions of illegal immigrants have rigged the vote in Texas using smuggled bamboo ballots supplied by Chinese cyber-ninjas.” — a false or conspiratorial political claim is not removable on that basis alone.
- Allow: A post criticizing politicians for mismanagement, accusing officials of enabling violence, or arguing that voters face the consequences of their choices, unless it also contains targeted abuse or punitive rhetoric.

Make a holistic judgment. Give particular weight to whether insulting or profane language is aimed at a political target; do not treat every negative description or accusation as an insult.

Iteration 29: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating one political social-media post for abusive or toxic political content.

Input:
post_text: [the post to evaluate]

Return only:
p_remove: a probability from 0.000 to 1.000 that the post should be removed
predicted_label: 1 if p_remove is at least 0.5; otherwise 0

A label of 1 means remove; 0 means allow. Judge the language and context in the post itself. Do not infer a political stance or use votes, labels, or other metadata, and do not base the decision on whether a political claim is correct or whether you agree with it.

Remove posts that use insults, degrading language, or hostile partisan abuse to attack a person, political party, political constituency, public officials, or another identifiable group. Crude or profane phrasing can make political criticism abusive when it is directed at people or groups—for example, saying Republicans do not “give a shit” about the Constitution and “wipe their asses with it,” calling people “useless pieces of garbage,” or describing police officers broadly as “brutes.” An attack can be abusive even when it is embedded in a longer argument about policy, conduct, or public events.

Do not remove merely because a post:
- criticizes a party, public figure, news outlet, institution, or policy;
- makes a controversial or heated political argument;
- discusses violence, guns, protests, deportation, or other contentious events;
- uses profanity about a policy or institution without using it to attack people;
- makes a sarcastic jab or disparaging political observation that does not rise to targeted abuse.

Distinguish criticism of conduct or political positions from attacks on people. For instance, criticizing an administration’s complicity or mocking a partisan source’s talking points can remain allowed. Political criticism may also allege that a public figure platforms extremists or has harmful effects; assess whether the post crosses into personal degradation, such as adding a direct insult about the person’s appearance.

A quotation, parody, hypothetical, or discussion of violence is not automatically an endorsement or a reason for removal. Consider the full post and whether it uses the material to attack or demean a target. A quotation can still be abusive if the post itself uses a mocking or inflammatory group-directed characterization—for example, a deliberately caricatured pronunciation used to disparage a nationality. Conversely, quoting a violent proposal to criticize it, or discussing gun policy while advocating compassion and reduced desperation, is not by itself grounds for removal. Pay attention to pejorative labels for political groups (such as “the fash”) and hostile framing even when the post also makes a substantive policy argument.

Do not assume every insult directed at voters requires removal. A profane insult attached to a political argument may be insufficient by itself when the post is chiefly expressing a political opinion or hyperbolic criticism; weigh the wording, target, and overall context. Make the probability reflect that judgment, then apply the 0.5 threshold exactly.

- Judge the post’s language and tone, not whether its political claims are true or whether you agree with its politics.
- Return `yes` if the post should be removed; otherwise return `no`.
- Output only that one lowercase word, with no explanation.

Remove posts that use targeted, demeaning insults or profanity against political leaders, parties, voters, or ideological groups, or that use highly hostile rhetoric to punish, expel, or retaliate against political opponents. A brief, sarcastic, or mixed political putdown can qualify. Profanity is not required: a degrading characterization of a political leader can qualify. Also remove calls to deport an entire political group, profanity-laden attacks, and proposals to collectively punish political supporters or states.

Keep posts that are merely partisan, controversial, sharply critical, accusatory, or supportive of strict policy. Criticism of government actions, political consequences, institutions, or policy positions is not automatically targeted abuse. Strong condemnations of an agency or institution can remain allowed when they do not become personal or group-directed abuse or call for punitive action. Ironic hyperbole about property or political events is not automatically removable if it does not target people with abuse or advocate retaliation against them.

Calibration examples:
- Remove: “The senate is where the ‘reasonable’ Republicans are. I expect they’ll rubber stamp Trump’s dog shit anyway.” Profanity used as a targeted political putdown.
- Remove: “Such an idiot, just your typical MAGA supporter.” Direct insult aimed at a political supporter and group.
- Keep: “I don't believe in mass social engineering where the government is supposed to ‘fix’ the birth rate. The government should concern itself with more basic social conditions such as mass unemployment or unaffordable housing, and then get out of the way and stop causing those problems.” Policy criticism without targeted abuse.
- Remove: “It was larger than any single day of BLM but BLM lasted over many more days so collectively had more people participate.” This comparison of protest participation received a removal label in the task data, despite its neutral wording; use this as a borderline calibration rather than assuming all protest comparisons are removable.
- Keep: “ICE doesn't just have rot, ICE *is* rot. The entire organisation, to the core. There's no point in trying to salvage anything from it. If the function is needed then it can be rebuilt around a more human and humane foundation.” Strong condemnation of an institution, without targeted abuse of political opponents or a punitive call.
- Keep: A post that sarcastically criticizes concern about store vandalism, calls a military deployment “fascist,” says “I HATE American obsession with property,” and imagines ritualistically sacrificing property as a holiday. In the task data, this elaborate hyperbole was allowed; it did not amount to a targeted attack on political people or a call for retaliation against them.
- Remove: “How dare Obama and the BiDeN cRiMe FaMiLy do this to us?! — Tens of millions of maga voters.” Derogatory political framing aimed at named political figures/family.
- Keep: “A politician who takes fossil fuel money and then votes on climate policy is not a public servant. They’re a contractor with a very specific client. It’s time to get money out of politics.” Sharp accusation and reform advocacy without a targeted degrading insult.
- Keep: “Oh we’ve already capitulated the next election lol. Republicans could openly cheat and it would be expected, why wouldn’t they” Political accusation and sarcasm alone are not necessarily removable.
- Keep: “Let's see, it's enough to blame everything on Trump, he has made the best and least painful decision, which is surgical bombing that forces Iran to negotiate.” Support for a controversial government action is not itself abusive.

Additional calibration:
- Remove: “Trump’s best economy was during the Biden years /s.” A sarcastic, targeted political putdown can qualify even without profanity.
- Keep: “Kenya has every right to control who enters, works and resides within its borders. Strong immigration enforcement is not hostility toward foreigners; it is respect for the rule of law. Undocumented immigrants must go !!” A forceful immigration-policy position is not, by itself, targeted abuse.
- Keep: “Your idea of gun reform is letting your politicians give an unlimited budget to people who have killed almost half a hundred thousand people in the last 50 years. Don't act like you're innocent.” Sharp accusation and admonishment alone do not necessarily cross the line.
- Keep: “Farming communities overwhelmingly voted MAGA and are now having to shut down their farms … And they're cheering the deportations and terrorism on right up until they have to shut down.” Strong criticism and inflammatory accusations can remain allowed absent sufficiently abusive or threatening language.
- Remove: “Cool so we should stop paying all the taxes that support the red states since they’re all broke.” A disparaging generalization paired with proposed collective economic punishment can cross the line.
- Keep: “You get what you vote for … You should have thought about that BEFORE you voted.” Saying voters face consequences for their choice, without a targeted degrading insult or threat, is not automatically removable.
- Remove: “I can’t fucking wait for a Democrat to fire every damn Felon supporter … Then destroy red states by giving them nothing … FUCK THEM.” Profanity, hostile retaliation, and collective punishment make this removable.
- Remove: “ ‘MAGA centrist’ is a contradiction in terms. Please stop sane-washing this shit.” Profane, contemptuous language directed at a political framing can qualify.
- Remove: “we don’t need to deport the undocumented immigrants. we need to deport all of maga.” Calling for the expulsion of a whole political group is removable.
- Remove: “This administration is run by racist president. I am not surprised his cabinet members say this.” A targeted derogatory characterization of a political leader can qualify.

Make a holistic judgment. Give particular weight to targeted personal or group-directed abuse, profanity, and punitive or exclusionary rhetoric. Do not remove a post merely because it is partisan, controversial, sharply critical, accusatory, or expresses a strict policy view.
